[Table_InudstryRankInfo] 行业投资评级 
强于大市：未来6个月内行业指数相对大盘涨幅10％以上； 
同步大市：未来6个月内行业指数相对大盘涨幅10％至-10％之间； 
弱于大市：未来6个月内行业指数相对大盘跌幅10％以上。

[Table_StockRankInfo] 公司投资评级 
买入：未来6个月内公司相对大盘涨幅15％以上； 
增持：未来6个月内公司相对大盘涨幅5％至15％； 
观望：未来6个月内公司相对大盘涨幅-5％至5％； 
卖出：未来6个月内公司相对大盘跌幅5％以上。 
基准指数 ：沪深300指数

日本：7月以降の設備投資需要はAI関連産業を中心に旺盛
3
 7～9月期の機械受注は増勢回復へ。AI関連業種からの受注がけん引
― ①旺盛なAI需要のもと、関連業種では設備不足感が強まっていたこと、②輸出増が持続するもとで、好業績が期待され
ること、③中東情勢の不確実性が後退したことなどが背景
 先行き設備投資は緩やかな増勢を辿る見込み
― 半導体製造装置をはじめ、AI・データセンター関連品目を中心に受注残が増加しており、目先こうした傾向が続く見通し
― 積み上がった受注残を段階的に消化することで、設備投資が持続的に下支えされる公算が大きい

世界：AI関連投資の拡大が鉱物需要を押し上げる一方、鈍い資源開発投資の回復ペース
4
 AI関連需要の拡大が銅やレアアースの需給環境に影響
― データセンター建設や電力インフラ整備に伴い、銅やレアアースの需要が拡大。ガリウム等の戦略鉱物への需要も増加
 資源開発投資の回復が遅れるなか、中長期的な供給不足への懸念強まる
―IEAは、2035年に銅が供給量対比で約26％の供給不足に陥ると予測。レアアースは供給不足を回避する見込みながら、

採掘・精錬において中国が支配的地位を占めており、供給元の集中がリスク
IEA：銅、レアアースの需要見通し IEA：銅・レアアースの需給バランス予測（2035年）
（出所）IEA "Global Critical Minerals Outlook 2026"より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
AI関連投資の拡大を背景に、
銅やレアアースの需要が拡大
銅は供給が不足へ
レアアースは供給超過も供給元の集中がリスク
注目点①AI投資拡大と資源供給制約

世界：AI投資が金融市場を下支えする一方、持続性への懸念は残存
 IMFはAI投資や企業収益の拡大を背景に金融市場が底堅さを維持していると評価
― ただし、金融安定リスクは引き続き高いとの評価で、AI、国債市場、サイバーを主要なリスク要因として指摘
 AIについてはAI主導の市場調整リスクを警戒
―AI中核企業の収益拡大によって株価の割高感は緩和する一方、社債発行や循環的な資金調達が拡大。投資収益が期
待を下回った場合、金融市場への影響が増幅される可能性
5

世界：国債市場に残る脆弱性。インフレ・財政懸念による金利急騰リスクは増大
 主要国の長期金利は、期待短期金利とタームプレミアムの双方から上昇
―IMFは財政懸念などを背景にタームプレミアムが歴史的に高い領域にあると指摘
― 国債発行の増加や借換リスクの高まりに加え、価格感応度の高い投資家の存在がインフレ・財政ショックによる金利上
昇を増幅する可能性
― 国債市場は足元で秩序を維持するも、急激な金利上昇は資金調達市場の逼迫や銀行部門への波及を招くリスク

### Page 8

世界：ヘッジファンドのレバレッジ拡大が市場ショックを増幅、銀行部門への波及にも警戒
 高まるヘッジファンドの存在感と市場への影響力
― ヘッジファンドの総資産は2013年の約4兆ドルから2026年初には約13兆ドルに拡大。平時には市場の流動性や効率性
を支える一方、ストレス時にはレバレッジ解消や取引集中が価格変動を増幅させる懸念
 ヘッジファンドの損失がプライムブローカーを通じて銀行部門に波及するリスク
―IMFはヘッジファンドの運用成績悪化がプライムブローカーの収益にも悪影響を及ぼすと分析。銀行自身の健全性に加
え、ノンバンクとの相互連関を通じた金融システム全体への波及にも警鐘
7
（注） グロス想定元本エクスポージャー：ヘッジファンドが保有するロング（買い）・ショート
（売り）両ポジションの想定元本の合計額。デリバティブも想定元本ベースで計上
（出所）IMF ”Global Financial Stability Report”(October 2026)より、みずほ総合研究所

欧州：EU・中国はハイブリッド車の輸入制限で暫定合意。同様の措置は他の品目に広がる可能性
11
 EUと中国は、貿易不均衡の是正措置について暫定合意。中国からのハイブリッド車輸入は従来見通しの半分以下に
―EUが中国産EVに追加関税を賦課した2024年以降、追加関税の対象とならないハイブリッド車輸入が急増
― 欧州委員会の試算では、今回の措置によって2030年までの4年間で数百万台の輸入を削減見込み。直近では、EUが輸
入するハイブリッド車の約半分を中国車が占めており、域内自動車市場に一定のインパクト
 欧州委員会は、幅広い産業で貿易不均衡を是正する必要性を示唆。同様の措置が他の品目にも広がる可能性
― 欧州委員会のモニタリング対象（輸入増加と価格下落が併存する品目）は、中国からの輸入品が最多

 フィリピンでAZEC（閣僚会合）とAEBFが同時期に開催。ASEANのエネルギー安全保障に関心が高まる

 ASEAN5では石油の国家備蓄制度や設備が未整備。石油の安定供給に向けた対応の強化が急務
―ASEANの場合、石油備蓄量は1～2カ月分と限定的。緊急時を見据えた石油備蓄の整備は途上
―10月のAZEC閣僚会合においては、ASEANの石油備蓄拡大や、緊急時の共同備蓄放出に向けた計画などで合意
 電力については域内での需要拡大が見込まれる中、発電能力の増強や送配電網の整備による電力供給の拡大が課題
―IEAは東南アジアの電力需要が2030年まで年平均5.4％増加すると予測。産業や冷房、データセンター需要が押し上げ
― 再エネ含む多様な電源による供給拡大や、AEBFでも議論された国際送配電網の整備や電力融通の取り組みが主眼に
アジア：ASEANでは石油備蓄の強化が喫緊の課題。将来の需要増に向けた電力確保も重要に

16

はIEAの見通しに基づき延長。

### Page 18

 ASEANの消費は中東情勢緊迫化後に減速も、概して底堅く推移
― インドネシア・タイは4～6月期に大きめに落ち込んだものの足元で持ち直しの動き。フィリピン、マレーシアは減速が続く
も大崩れを回避、ベトナムは堅調さを維持
 背景に各国個別要因や消費マインド悪化の歯止め
― タイは消費刺激策（6～11月）、マレーシアはインフレの抑制、ベトナムは高い賃金上昇率が消費を下支え
― 消費マインドは中東情勢緊迫化後の悪化に歯止め。もっとも、中東危機の長期化が重しに
アジア：ASEANの消費は足元で総じて底堅い動き

日本：コメ価格下落で企業物価は鈍化。もっとも、先行きは原油高を背景に再加速へ
18
 9月の国内企業物価はコメ価格の下落を主因に、前年比＋6.8％（前月：同＋7.6％）と鈍化
― 新米価格が昨年を下回ったこと等を背景に、コメ価格が大幅に下落。国内企業物価を▲0.75%Pt押し下げ
― 一方、3月来の原油高を背景に化学・プラスチック製品は高止まり。また、AI需要等を背景に非鉄金属も高い伸びを維持
 7月以降の原油高再燃を背景に、先行きの国内企業物価は再加速する見込み
― 原油価格高止まりにタンカー運賃の急騰が重なり、先行きの輸入物価は原油関連を中心に加速へ
― 国内企業物価は石油・石炭製品やラグを伴いつつ化学・プラスチック製品などを中心に再加速する見込み

Document 058: 2026年9月财政数据快评：收入向下，力度向上

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年9月财政数据快评：收入向下，力度向上.pdf
Report source: 国信证券
Date: 2026-10-25
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

事项：
前三季度，全国一般公共预算收入约 17.2万亿元，同比增长 5.1%。其中，全国税收收入约 14.1万亿元，
同比增长 6.1%。全国一般公共预算支出约 21.0万亿元，同比增长 1.0%。
评论：
9 月财政收入增速下滑，但政策力度指数继续回升。
第一本账收入转负，支出基本持平。9月收入同比下降 0.1%，较前值（4.7%）回落转负；税收收入中增值
税、个人所得税为主要拉动，消费税仍为最大单项拖累，企业所得税、证券交易印花税转负。支出当月同
比 0.03%，基本持平。后续随着基数抬高，收入将延续回落，支出端则预计逐渐向着预算增速追赶。
第二本账收入降幅再度扩大，支出降幅收窄。9 月第二本账收入同比-15.9%，土地出让收入同比-23.2%，
降幅均再度扩大，后续随着地产新政落地可能难有较大回升；支出同比下降 12.3%，降幅明显收窄，主要
是土地以外的其他支出降幅明显收窄，反映债务资金拨付有所加快。
总体来看，广义财政收支增速开始相向而行，力度指数连续回升。9月广义收入当月同比转负、广义支出

降幅明显收窄，二者增速差显著收窄，广义财政缺口同比收缩幅度也有所放缓。四季度只要增速达到 4.2%
以上，全年经济增速就能完成 4.5%的目标，5500 亿债务限额下达后，再出台增量政策的必要性并不算太
强。
风险提示：海外市场动荡，存在不确定性。
 一般公共预算：收入转负、支出持平
2026年10月24日

### Page 2

9 月财政收入小幅转负。1-9 月财政收入累计同比 5.1%，前值（1-8月）5.7%，有所回落。其中税收收入
累计 6.1%，前值 6.6%。单月来看，一般公共预算收入当月同比-0.1%，前值 4.7%，小幅转负。税收收入当
月同比 0.2%，前值 5.6%，连续第二个月回落；非税收入当月同比-1.0%，前值 0.3%，再度转负。税收显著
回落，主要是去年基数继续抬高叠加企业所得税等主体税种走弱；非税收入短暂转正后再度小幅负增长，
收入端整体进入趋势性回落通道，预计后续可能会进一步回落，逐渐靠近年初预算的收入目标（2.2%）。
图1：一般公共预算收入累计与当月增速 图2：一般公共收入月度进度
资料来源:WIND，国信证券经济研究所整理 资料来源:WIND，国信证券经济研究所整理
图3：税收收入与非税收入增速 图4：各税种同比增速与前值对比
资料来源:Wind，国信证券经济研究所整理 资料来源:Wind，国信证券经济研究所整理
主要税种增速分化，增值税回升、消费税仍为最大拖累。主要税种中增值税同比 7.3%，较前值（4.0%）回升，

拉动税收收入 3.1个百分点、为最大拉动项；个人所得税同比 10.5%，较前值（11.8%）小幅回落，拉动税
收 1.1个百分点，累计同比 14.1%，仍在高速增长区间；企业所得税同比-0.4%，较前值（11.6%）明显回
落转负；消费税同比-8.5%，降幅较前值（-14.0%）收窄，拖累税收 1.0个百分点、仍为最大的单项拖累。
房地产相关税种整体降幅由-1.9%明显扩大至-14.6%，其中契税（-16.9%）仍然疲弱，土地增值税（-22.2%）
再度转负。此外，证券交易印花税同比-11.5%（前值 17.9%），在 2025 年 9 月同比 342%的超高基数上转
负，拖累税收 0.3 个百分点，后续可能随成交量走弱持续降低；进口环节增值税和消费税同比 12.9%，拉
动税收 1.9个百分点，与增速较高的进口数据较为一致。

### Page 3

图5：各税种对税收收入的拉动排序 图6：主要税种同比增速
资料来源:Wind，国信证券经济研究所整理 资料来源:Wind，国信证券经济研究所整理
支出端与上月基本持平。一般公共支出当月同比 0.03%，较前值（-0.1%）小幅回升。节奏上，一般公共预
算支出当月完成 9.6%，与去年同期（9.7%）基本一致。累计来看，1-9月第一本账支出增速为 1.0%，累计
进度 70.0%，与去年同期（70.1%）大体相当；但离 4.4%的预算支出增速水平差距仍然较大，后续有待存
量资金加快拨付带动支出增速回升，全年增速预计回升到 2-3%之间。
结构上，民生支出转负、基建降幅收窄。民生类支出同比增速-0.9%，较前值（1.9%）回落转负。其中，
教育支出同比增长 3.5%，前值 1.3%；社保就业支出-4.8%，前值 2.6%；卫生健康支出同比-2.9%，较前值
（3.8%）回落；文化娱乐体育传媒支出增速 1.6%，由负转正（前值-9.6%）。基建类支出同比增速-1.3%，
前值-3.7%，拖累支出 0.3个百分点。其中交通运输支出同比-10.8%，前值-5.8%，降幅扩大；城乡社区事

务支出增速由前值-3.8%转正至 7.9%；农林水事务支出同比-0.2%，较前值（1.1%）小幅回落；环保支出-13.7%，
降幅继续收窄（前值-15.8%）。此外，债务付息支出同比-1.2%，较前值（7.0%）转负；科学技术支出同
比 6.9%，较前值（9.0%）回落，仍维持较快增长。

### Page 4

图9：各支出类型同比增速与前值对比 图10：各支出类型对支出的拉动

图11：民生与基建支出当月同比 图12：财政支出的季节性
资料来源:Wind，国信证券经济研究所整理 资料来源:Wind，国信证券经济研究所整理
 政府性基金预算：收入降幅再度扩大、支出降幅收窄
9 月政府性基金收入同比-15.9%（前值-3.8%），降幅再度扩大。土地出让收入（-23.2%）拖累重新加重，
同样较前值（-12.6%）明显扩大。政府性基金支出同比-12.3%，降幅较前值（-21.4%）明显收窄，主要是
除土地出让收入相关支出外的其他支出降幅由-18.0%收窄至-8.4%，反映专项债、特别国债资金拨付有所
加快。
1-9月，第二本账收入累计增速为-18.6%，支出增速为-16.2%。其中土地出让收入 16087 亿，同比-27.9%，
降幅较 1-8月（-28.6%）收窄，但仍处深度负增长区间，按当前降幅外推，全年仍不及 3 万亿。后续来看，

随着专项债、超长期特别国债资金拨付加快与新型政策性金融工具持续投放，第二本账支出端有望延续修
复。

### Page 5

图13：政府性基金收支增速 图14：土地使用权出让收入与支出增速
资料来源:Wind，国信证券经济研究所整理 资料来源:Wind，国信证券经济研究所整理
 总结：财政力度指数继续回升
财政力度指数连续第二个月回升。一方面是收入转负的同时支出降幅收窄。9月广义支出增速-3.7%，较前
值-6.7%明显收窄；广义收入当月同比增速-3.5%，前值 2.9%，由正转负。1-9月，广义财政支出累计增速
为-3.5%，完成度 65.2%，低于去年同期（67.1%）；广义财政收入累计增速为 1.4%，完成度 70.8%。另一
方面是赤字端同比收缩幅度放缓。1-9 月累计的广义财政缺口为 7.6万亿，较 2025年同期（8.8万亿）减
少约 1.3 万亿，同比收缩 14.4%，收缩幅度较 1-8 月（17.7%）有所收窄。此外，9 月新增财政存款-4300
亿，季末支出加快带动财政存款季节性回落，月末财政存款余额 7.9万亿；当月社融口径政府债券净融资
1.54万亿，较前值（1.01 万亿）明显回升。9月财政力度指数回升，连续第二个月回升。

后续来看，财政收支的 K 型分化将继续收敛。9月广义财政收入与支出增速已开始相向而行，政府债净融
资明显回升，8000 亿新型政策性金融工具持续投放，四季度专项债和超长期特别国债剩余额度发行使用仍
在途中。预计广义财政支出增速有望继续回升，关注政府债资金使用节奏与实物工作量形成情况。此外，
四季度只要增速达到 4.2%以上，全年经济增速就能完成 4.5%的目标，出台增量政策的必要性并不算太强，
现在的问题并非缺钱而是落地端。

2026 年1 月16 日

金融数据的三个边际改善 
——2026 年 9 月金融数据点评

事件：9 月新增社融 3.55 万亿，同比多增 192 亿元，存量社融
增速 7.1%，较上月下降 0.1 个百分点；金融机构新增 人民币贷款
1.08 万亿，同比少增2100 亿元，金融机构各项人民币贷款余额增速
4.8%，较上月下降 0.1 个百分点；M1 同比增长 3.2%，较上月下降
0.9 个百分点，M2 同比增长7.2%，较上月下降 0.3 个百分点。 
政府债券 发行提速 ，支撑 9 月社融同比多增。 9 月新增社融
3.55 万亿，同比多增 192 亿元，社融存量增速为 7.1%，较上月下降
0.1 个百分点。从新增社融结构来看，9 月新增政府债券融资 1.53
万亿，高于季节性 水平（2021-2025 年同期均值，下同 ），同比多
增 3438 亿元，是本月社融同比多增的重要支撑，表明财政发力节奏
加快、为四季度基建投资回升提供资金基础；企业债券融资减少
425 亿元、股票融资新增658 亿元，合计同比少增402 亿元，企业直
接融资走弱，可能与近期股市阶段性调整有关。间接融资方面， 9
月新增人民币贷款1.27 万亿，显著低于季节性水平（2.09 万亿），
同比少增 3342 亿元；人民币持续升值下，新增外币贷款 349 亿元，
同比多增478 亿元；新增三项非标2477 亿元，同比少增1102 亿元，
但其中，新型政策性金融工具投放拉动下，9 月新增委托贷款802 亿
元，同比多增 519 亿元。从前三季度整体情况来看，新增社融规模
基本持平于季节性水平，但内部结构发生较大变化，人民币贷款少
增，政府债券、企业直接融资多增，直接间接融资分化加剧。 今年
前三季度新增社融 27.46 万亿，基本持平于季节性水平（27.52 万
亿）。但从结构来看，新增人民币贷款 11.5 万亿，新增政府债券
10.3 万亿，新增企业债券和股票融资合计1.23 万亿，分别较季节性
水平减少 5.33 万亿、增加 3.31 万亿和增加 0.84 万亿。直接、间接
融资一增一减下，9 月末直接融资在新增社融中占比达 49.48%，较
去年同期上升 5.1 个百分点，而新增人民币贷款占比则较去年同期
下降6.45 个百分点至41.88%。 
金融数据点评 2026 年10 月15 日  2026 年第57 期

9 月金融数据点评： 
金融数据的三个边际改善 
居民信贷零新增，企业新增信贷低于季节性水平。9 月金融机构新增人民币贷款 1.08 万
亿，同比少增 2100 亿元，连续 13 个月同比少增。从结构来看，居民部门新增短期、中长期
信贷一负一正。9 月居民部门新增人民币贷款 0 亿元，同比少增 3890 亿元，其中，短期贷款
减少 900 亿元，同比少增 2321 亿元，已连续三个月同比少增，一是居民收入预期疲弱，消费
意愿不强；二是补贴政策退坡后汽车零售减少，根据乘联会数据， 9 月全国乘用车市场零售
161.8 万辆，同比下降 28.0%，9 月“旺季不旺”，或导致汽车消费贷减少；三是经营贷利率
上调、银行不再硬性考核普惠小微贷款增速、经营贷不良率攀升，共同导致经营贷规模缩减，
9 月短期经营贷同比少增约1500 亿元。9 月居民部门新增中长期贷款874 亿元，同比少增1626
亿元，与地产销售数据表现一致，9 月 30 大中城市商品房成交面积同比下降 10.75%，“金九”
成色不佳。企业新增信贷同比由负转正。9 月企业部门新增人民币贷款 1.29 万亿，同比多增
700 亿元，结束了连续三个月的同比少增。这主要源于票据融资基数较低，9 月票据融资规模
减少 500 亿元，低基数下同比少减 3526 亿元；从短期、中长期贷款来看，企业 9 月新增短期、
中长期贷款分别为 4600 亿元和 8900 亿元，分别同比少增 2500 亿元和 200 亿元，但中长期贷
款少增幅度显著收缩。从前三季度整体情况来看，1-9 月新增人民币贷款11.52 万亿，同比少
增 3.23 万亿，其中，居民部门同比少增 2.13 万亿，企业部门同比少增 0.89 万亿。实体信贷
需求仍然偏弱，但降幅已开始收窄。 从居民部门来看，三季度居民部门中长期贷款同比少增
2750 亿元，少增幅度较一、二季度均显著收窄。从企业部门来看，三季度企业新增贷款中，
中长期贷款占比开始上升，9 月该占比已上升至52.03%。

9 月金融数据点评： 
金融数据的三个边际改善 
M1、M2 增速均边际下行，M2-M1 剪刀差走扩。9 月 M1 同比增长 3.2%，较上月下降 0.9
个百分点，或主要源于去年同期基数较高。去年9 月M1 新增1.92 万亿，显著高于2021-2025
年同期均值（4339.6 亿元），而本月 M1 新增 9822 亿元，本身并不低。从各部门来看，9 月
住户活期存款余额较上月上升、非银存款余额较上月下降，资本市场收益率下降下居民风险
偏好下降，“存款搬家”暂缓；非银企业活期存款余额结束了连续两个月环比下降的趋势，
较上月增长5556 亿元，表明9 月进入生产旺季后，企业开支需求增加，但持续性仍待观察；
财政存款余额较上月下降 4360 亿元，财政支出力度加大。9 月 M2 同比增长 7.2%，较上月下
降0.3 个百分点，主要源于贷款增速下降，阻碍货币派生。

9 月金融数据点评： 
金融数据的三个边际改善 
9 月人民币汇率整体走强，月中突破 6.70 关键点位。9 月人民币在岸汇率在 6.6955 至
6.7219 间波动，人民币离岸汇率在6.6927 至6.7223 间波动，在岸、离岸人民币报价无显著
背离趋势。月初至 21 日，欧、美、日等主要经济体集体上调基准利率，但人民币仍呈现出
震荡上行的独立行情，并于 21 日突破了 6.70 的关键点位，在岸、离岸人民币汇率分别达到
6.6955 和6.6927，创2023 年2 月以来新高。9 月下旬美元兑人民币汇率掉头回升至6.70 以
上，30 日，在岸、离岸人民币汇率分别为6.7053 和6.7088。19 日，中国人民银行货币政策
委员会召开 2026 年第三季度例会，汇率方面新增强调“坚持市场在汇率形成中的决定性 作
用” ，以及“防范市场‘羊群效应’和非理性预期的自我强化” ，释放稳汇率基调。从前三季
度尤其是 9 月人民币汇率走势来看，结汇需求而非中美利差已成为影响人民币汇率定价的主
要因素。1-9 月，美元指数整体上涨 3%，中美利差（美国 10 年期国债收益率-中国 10 年期
国债收益率）较年初上升约 128 个基点至 3.6%，但人民币汇率却上涨了约 4%，表明中美利
差倒挂不再是影响人民币汇率的主要因素，人民币汇率上行的主要动力在于结汇需求旺盛。

数据来源：Wind,中诚信国际

9 月金融数据点评： 
金融数据的三个边际改善 
1-9 月，我国出口同比增长21.3%，贸易顺差为9381 亿美元，催生了大量结汇需求，1-8 月，
我国银行代客结汇金额为13.97 万亿元、同比增长26.3%，代客结售汇差额为2.74 万亿元、
较去年同期增长了 7.6 倍。此外，人民币升值预期也提升了结汇需求，反过来又推动人民币
汇率进一步上行，形成了人民币汇率的自我强化，这也是央行强调“防范市场‘羊群效应’
和非理性预期的自我强化”的重要原因。

9 月金融数据点评： 
金融数据的三个边际改善 
整体来看，9 月金融数据呈现出一定的边际改善。 一是财政发力显著，9 月新增政府债
券融资规模同比、环比上升，财政存款余额环比下降；二是企业融资表现有所改善，9 月委
托贷款同比多增，新型政策性金融工具发力见效，企业新增贷款同比多增，且新增企业贷款
中中长期贷款占比边际上升，同时企业部门活期存款余额由降转增，均表明企业开支和融资
意愿增强；三是居民新增中长期贷款转正，且三季度新增中长期贷款同比降幅较一、二季度
明显收窄。但需要注意的是，在内需不足和预期偏弱的背景下，实体部门融资需求边际改善
的持续性仍需政策呵护。展望后续，四季度实体部门融资需求有望温和改善，社融增速或小
幅回升。其一，9 月底 PSL 降息扩围、增加科创与技术改造再贷款与支农支小再贷款额度、
居民购房贷款贴息等一系列政策效果有望在四季度显现；其二，四季度地方债发行有望提速，
1-9 月，地方政府新增一般债、地方债发行进度均低于去年和过去三年同期水平，四季度仍
有较大空间；其三，财政部近日表示安排使用地方政府债务结存限额 5500 亿元，用于提高
县区一般公共预算保障能力和支持地方扩大有效投资 ，有望缓解地方财政收支矛盾并托底四
季度及明年初基建投资；其四，今年 8000 亿元新型政策性金融工具于 9 月开始投放，目前
国开新型政策性金融工具已投放资金约600 亿元，后续仍有约7000 亿规模有待使用；其五，
9.28 国常会明确加大逆周期调节力度，并推出一批务实管用的增量政策，后续根据经济运行
情况，降准、增发超长期特别国债、增加政策性金融工具额度等政策有望落地。 
汇率方面，9 月人民币汇率震荡上行，四季度人民币或将双向浮动为主、温 和升值为辅。
一方面，中国经济保持韧性，9 月制造业 PMI 指数回升至荣枯线以上，同时，中国制造业持
续升级叠加全球 AI 浪潮延续，出口仍有一定支撑，企业结汇需求仍存。但另一方面，央行
明确需防范“羊群效应” ，后续若人民币汇率升值过快，央行或将加大调控力度。此外，美

9 月金融数据点评：
金融数据的三个边际改善
国 9 月非农、通胀数据的反向指示表明今年美联储或仍有一次加息，也将对人民币汇率造成
一定扰动。整体来看，四季度人民币汇率或将保持双向浮动，若出口和结汇需求较为强劲，
尤其年末企业结汇需求抬升，人民币汇率或将小幅升值。另外，央行在近期发布的《中国人
民银行关于人民币汇率的政策立场》表明，中国无意通过人民币贬值提升出口竞争力，也不
会因为外部压力推动人民币升值，而是坚持让市场在汇率形成中发挥决定性作用，保持汇率
弹性和双向浮动，降低了汇率政策的不确定性，稳定了市场预期。

營 運 分 析

南亞科技為全球前四大 DRAM廠商 
南亞科技主要致力 DRAM (動態隨機存取記憶體)的研發、設計、製造與銷售，
開發自主技術，發展智慧財產權，培育關鍵技術研發團隊，落實綠色科技及
智慧製造全面提升製程效能與產能，是全球第4大的 DRAM公司。公司已成
功開發 10 奈米級 DRAM 製程技術，將可微縮至少 3 個世代，並用以發展
DDR5及 LPDDR5 等下世代產品，強化公司自主技術開發與製程轉換佈局。
第二世代 10 奈米級製程技術(1B)產品 16Gb DDR5 已完成設計與試產，於
2024 年量產。南亞科技具備完整 DRAM 產品線，可因應智慧世代的發展，
滿足多元化的產品需求。預期 1C產品於 27Q1 開始驗證。

圖三、DRAM合約價報價走勢圖

資料來源：兆豐投顧整理

南亞科完成 787.2 億元私募案，攜手四大客戶搶佔 AI 供應鏈先機 
南亞科日前完成總額新台幣  787.2 億元的私募認購，吸引四大核心客戶參
與。增資後，相關投資方持股比例達 10.19%。雙方同時簽署供應協議，確
保未來能穩定供應多元 DRAM 產品組合。主要目的在於強化南亞科與客戶
的戰略合作，透過 e-SSD 與網通產品等領域，建立 AI 及雲端服務供應商
（CSP）供應鏈中的雙贏局面。私募資金將注資新廠以提升產能，進一步確
立南亞科在 AI 價值鏈中的市場地位。本次私募後，南亞科總發行股數將達 
34.5 億股。

記憶體供不應求，產能持續擴充 
目前產能為 6.5 萬片/月，產能利用率維持滿載，2026Q1~Q3 資本支出 276
億，2026 以不超過 697 億元為上限，設備佔比五成．5A新廠第一階段 35,900
片/月 於 2026~2029 資本支出約 3,466 億．新廠預計於 2027 年初開始裝機，
主要生產 1B/1C/1D製程，預期 27年底開出 20k/月，2028 年達每月投片量
達 30k/月，主要因應網通、eSSD等產品之強勁需求。

AI 帶動記憶體強勁需求，有利報價持續走升 
展望 2026~2027 年，受惠 AI 需求持續強勁三大原廠於 DRAM 產能持續滿
載，預期DDR5、DDR4、DDR3等產品將持續供不應求。1C產品預期於 27Q1
開始驗證，有利打入 AI 伺服器供應鏈，預期 AI 占比將逐季提高。因此兆豐
預估營收 4208.9/6993.1 億元，YoY+532.1/66.2%，毛利率83.3/86.3%，EPS
為 77.85/133.2 元。

求，報價持續走升。

於生產 1B/1C/1D 製

2026 OCP Global Summit –
MGX Upgrades Take Shape;
Maia 300 on Display
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+
Howard Kao

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
At the rack level, Maia 300 is the key incremental story, while
Nvidia/AMD exhibits offered limited new system-level hardware.
Nvidia’s MGX component upgrades are the main supply-chain
watch points, although PTFE and next-gen interconnect and
cooling connector (ZQD) adoption remains uncertain.

Nvidia/AMD –  focus on ramps rather than new platforms. Most ODM displays
were similar to Computex Taipei (early June), so timing of ramp is key. For Nvidia,
VR200 racks have started a small-volume ramp, with the Nvidia Groq 3 LPX rack
following in late 2026/early 2027 and standalone Vera CPU racks in late 1Q27/early
2Q27, based on our checks. For AMD, Helios remained largely unchanged with
recent checks, with racks ramping in 1Q27, with pilot-build observations pointing to

Shennan Circuits for EAM PCBs and Fositek/Parker for quick disconnects.
Maia 300 -  a larger in-house inference opportunity. Microsoft plans production
deployments in 2027 as it scales Maia toward multi-gigawatt deployments. Each
rack integrates 96 accelerators within 167 kW. Based on that, each gigawatt of
deployment would imply 5 to 5.5K racks, and we believe Hon Hai and Wiwynn could
be the ODM partners, while our pilot-build observations point to ISU Petasys for the

accelerator PCB, AVC for fans, and Fositek/Staubli for quick disconnects.
PTFE - technical upside, but no confirmed timing of adoption. Nvidia is evaluating
PTFE for Rubin Ultra on Oberon following the Kyber delay and showcased its
NVLink PTFE PCB with seven suppliers (though we think there are suppliers missing
from this list). Better signal performance and reduced reliance on Q glass are
potential benefits, but processing complexity, higher costs and yield/qualification

risks remain hurdles to commercial adoption.
Ultra Dense Interconnect - next-generation interconnect opportunity for FIT.
Nvidia showcased six suppliers for this next-generation interconnect, which is
intended to replace Amphenol’s current HD2 connector in midplane and cable
cartridge applications. However, we believe adoption is still about two years away. In
the meantime, the supply chain is working to improve yields and bring ASPs below
US$100, with pogo-pin supply currently a bottleneck. Our checks identify FIT as the

key design partner for Kyber’s planned board-to-board connector, a position that
bodes well for its supply share once the product ramps.
ZQD - higher flow density and an expected ASP premium. Nvidia’s new ZQD
targets higher flow rates per unit area and was displayed with 14 suppliers.
Although the supply chain is working to improve yields and lower costs, greater
design complexity should keep ASPs above current QD pricing of US$20–30.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
October 19, 2026 01:16 AM GMT

Major Takeaways from OCP Summit 2026
ODM/OEM: Almost all the ODM/OEMs had a booth at OCP, but during our visit, we feel
most of the products were largely similar to what was showcased at Computex, such as
the Vera Rubin NVL72 rack, the LPX tray, the Vera standalone tray. So net, we think from a
hardware perspective, there was nothing new on Nvidia or AMD. We believe the Nvidia
VR200 rack is already starting ramp in small volume, with the LPX rack starting to ramp

later this year or early next year, and the Vera standalone CPU rack ramping late 1Q27 or
early 2Q27.
AMD: Wiwynn, Meta, and AMD each showcased the Helios rack, which was unchanged
from last year’s OCP display with one exception: this year’s version featured the actual
compute tray in place of last year’s dummy tray, allowing a closer look. That said, the
compute tray itself was not new to us, as we had already seen it at OCP Taiwan in August.
• Based on our observations at the OCP Summit, we believe Shennan Circuits is one

of the pilot build PCB suppliers for the MI455 Helios EAM PCB.
• Based on our observations at the OCP Summit, we believe Fositek (MQD02) and
Parker (UQD04) are one of the pilot build quick disconnect connector suppliers for
the MI455 Helios compute tray.
• HPE displayed the Helios switch tray at the OCP Summit. We believe the other
supplier of the Helios switch trays is Celestica.
Microsoft: Maia 300 represents a major expansion of Microsoft’s in-house inference

infrastructure. Announced on October 12, 2026, with production deployments planned for
2027, it forms part of Microsoft’s commitment to scale the Maia family to multi-gigawatt
deployments and make it the primary inference platform for its products and services. The
design targets long-context reasoning, conversational AI, and multi-step agentic
workloads, with an emphasis on improving inference economics through coordinated
silicon, software, and system design.

Maia 300 rack integrates 96 accelerators within a 167 kW power envelope, providing
approximately 27.6 TB of aggregate HBM capacity. There are 4 accelerators per tray,
implying 24 compute trays per rack, including six high-radix switch ASICs that are
integrated into three liquid-cooled switch trays, providing the rack-local connectivity
required by all 96 accelerators. Microsoft projects 40% more tokens per megawatt than
leading GPU-based systems and up to 7× Maia 200’s rack-level token throughput, though

this takes into account that the Maia 300 rack  includes twice as many accelerators per
rack.
• The Official Microsoft Blog A two-tier Ethernet scale-up network connects up to
128 racks, or 12,288 accelerators, while liquid cooling removes more than 80% of
rack heat. Each compute tray includes 8x fans  (Exhibit 3 ), and based on our
observations at the OCP Summit, we believe AVC is one of the pilot build fan
suppliers for the sample compute tray.

• Based on our observations at the OCP Summit, we believe ISU Petasys is one of
the pilot build PCB suppliers for Microsoft’s Maia 300 AI accelerator.
• Based on our observations at the OCP Summit, we believe Fositek and Staubli are
pilot build MQD03 (quick disconnect connector) suppliers for the compute tray.

• With each rack being 167 kW of power, one gigawatt of deployment would imply 5-
5.5K racks, assuming a PUE of 1.1-1.2.

Nvidia MGX Ecosystem
PTFE: We previously wrote that our supply chain checks suggest that Nvidia had initially
planned to adopt PTFE CCL for the very high-layer-count midplane in its Kyber
architecture. With Kyber now delayed, and Rubin Ultra no longer expected to use that
architecture, as previously discussed, the focus has shifted back to the Oberon
architecture. Nvidia is again evaluating PTFE for Rubin Ultra on Oberon, and at this year’s

OCP Summit, Nvidia showcased its NVLink PTFE PCB, with a total of 7 suppliers, including
VGT, TTM, Taiflex, SYTECH, Shennan Circuits, Kinwong, and Flexium (Exhibit 4  & Exhibit
5 ). However, our checks indicate that actual adoption for Rubin Ultra remains uncertain.
However, we find the supplier list puzzling. Our checks suggest that WUS PCB and
Unimicron are also working on this solution, and that Flexium, which has collaborated
with other PCB suppliers, may handle only a few process steps. Our checks also indicate

that Shennan Circuits, WUS PCB and VGT are now the leading PCB makers for this
solution, with relatively higher yields.
Advantages of PTFE
• Best signal performance: the lowest Dk and Df of the mainstream options, below
even standard M9-class CCLs. This matters most where insertion-loss budgets are
tightest, i.e. the highest speeds and highest layer counts.
• Stable electricals: the low Dk/Df hold up across frequency and temperature, and
PTFE absorbs very little moisture, so performance is stable in use.

• Less dependence on Q glass: because PTFE’s intrinsic dielectric constant is already
very low, reaching the target Dk requires less (and in some hybrid constructions,
none) of the expensive, supply-constrained Q glass. That is both a cost lever and a
supply-security benefit.
Disadvantages of PTFE

### Page 5

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 5
• Mechanically soft: low modulus makes it difficult to handle and to hold to
tolerance.
• Difficult lamination: PTFE does not flow like a thermoset, so bonding needs either
very high temperatures or lower-melting fluoropolymer (such as PFA
(polyfluoroalkoxy))/thermoset bond layers; its high, non-linear CTE stresses
copper adhesion and complicates layer-to-layer registration, and pressing typically
requires vacuum or an inert atmosphere with longer cycles.

• Difficult drilling: the soft, gummy resin tends to smear and can tear, and any
ceramic filler is abrasive on tooling. Also, the hole wall will not accept electroless
copper without an added activation step (sodium etch or plasma) on top of normal
desmear.
• Higher cost and qualification risk: materials and fabrication are both more
expensive, the qualified supply base is narrower, and yields at very high layer
counts carry risk.
Exhibit 4: Nvidia's NVLink PTFE Board

Source: Nvidia, Morgan Stanley Research.

### Page 6

M
Idea
6
Exhibit 5: Nvidia PTFE PCB Supplier List
Source: Nvidia, Morgan Stanley Research.
Ultra Dense Interconnect: Currently, Nvidia's NVL72 racks are using Amphenol's Paladin
HD2 connectors for VR200 NVL72 midplanes and cable cartridges. Our previous checks
done around Computex 2026 in June, indicate ASP could reach US$70-90+ per set. For
the next-generation Kyber racks, our checks have previously indicated that a new midplane
connector called "Voronoi" will be introduced, and FIT is the key design partner for this

new board-to-board connector. At this year’s OCP Summit, Nvidia showcased a new
connector it calls the “Ultra Dense Interconnect”, with six suppliers in total. This was our
first in-person look at the design. We believe adoption is still two years out, and the
supply chain is currently focused on improving yields and optimizing costs to bring the
ASP below US$100. Our checks suggest that pogo pin supply is currently a bottleneck for
this connector.

### Page 7

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 7
Exhibit 6: Nvidia next-generation Ultra Dense Interconnect
Source: Nvidia, Morgan Stanley Research.
ZQD: At COMPUTEX in Taipei earlier this year, we noted that our checks indicated Nvidia
was planning to introduce a new quick disconnect (QD) design for next-generation server
racks to achieve higher flow rates per unit area as cooling requirements rise. At this year’s
OCP Summit, Nvidia showcased that design, called ZQD, with 14 suppliers in total. Our

checks indicate the supply chain is also working to improve yields and optimize costs to
bring ZQD ASPs down. However, given the greater complexity of the design, we expect
ZQD ASPs to remain above current QD pricing of US$20–30 per set.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Document 132: 38美元，DeepSeek算了一笔赚钱的账

Source PDF: all report pdf/38美元，DeepSeek算了一笔赚钱的账.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

DeepSeek 最新披露⼀个震撼数字：单 GPU 每⼩时可创造 38 美元计费产值，年化超 33 万美
元。但这⻔⽣意真的赚钱吗？⼀边是中⼩企业 AI 需求 " 全⾯暴涨 " ，⼀边是 OpenAI 、
Anthropic 被推理成本侵蚀的⽑利率；⼀边是 CoreWeave112% 的营收增速，⼀边是⾼达
6.26 亿美元的季度净亏损。效率提升与商业盈利之间，横亘着⼀道尚未解答的命题。
摘要：
10 ⽉ 20 ⽇， DeepSeek 开源推理框架 DeepInfer 时，公布了⼀个⼗分吸引眼球的数字：单个集群
每天可以处理 155 万亿 Token ，如果按照 DeepSeek API 的⾕时价格计算，平均每 GPU ⼩时对应
38 美元的收⼊。
这是根据 Token 处理量和公开 API 价格折算出的理论计费收⼊，实际情况要复杂得多。但这个数
字依然太有吸引⼒：如果粗暴按照线性外推，按每天 24 ⼩时、全年 365 天计算，⼀张 GPU 的年
化计费产值超过 33 万美元。
过去⼏年， AI ⾏业⼀直在讨论训练⼀个前沿模型需要多少钱，英伟达的 GPU 有多贵，云⼚商⼜

投⼊了多少资⾦建设数据中⼼。但这些昂贵的计算设备买回来之后，究竟能够⽣产多少可以卖
钱的智能？抛开眼花缭乱的 “ ⾦融⼿段 ” ，企业真能赚到的，⼜有多少钱？
DeepSeek 的 38 美元，提供了⼀个难得的观察窗⼝。在资本市场再次对 AI 巨额投⼊的回报提出质
疑的时候，产业⼀线的反馈却很乐观。 “ 业内 ” 和 “ 业外 ” ，产⽣了巨⼤的分歧。
⼀位经营 Token 中转业务的创业者告诉腾讯科技，最近⼏个⽉，中⼩企业的 AI 需求正在 “ 全⾯暴
涨 ”，他能从客⼾的实际使⽤情况中感受到这⼀变化。今年七⼋⽉还只有少数公司在尝试的事
情，如今同⾏已经普遍在做。⼀些原本有数据安全顾虑的企业也开始私有化部署模型。他也在
认真考虑 “ 扩产 ” 。
另外⼀位 AI 应⽤领域的创业者态度也⼗分鲜明： “ 英伟达的股票，我还是会继续买。 ” 他接触的 B 端
企业正在加快引⼊ Agent ，从⽇常运营到客⼾服务，越来越多业务开始 AI 化。
但他也判断，中美 AI 市场短期内可能经历⼀个 “ ⼩低⾕ ” 。此前模型突破、⽣产率提升和 AI 替代⼯

作的消息不断推⾼市场预期，当资本市场开始要求 AI 在真⾦⽩银上交出成绩时，企业应⽤的实际
成果可能还没有跟上，市场热情也会随之回落。
这或许正是当下 AI 产业⾯临的⽭盾：⼀线需求正在增⻓，但从应⽤落地到形成⾜以⽀撑巨额算⼒
投⼊的商业回报，中间还有⼀段距离。
DeepSeek 的计算提供了另⼀个值得关注的信号，即使按照⾕时价格，⾼度优化的推理系统仍然
能够实现相当可观的单位 GPU 计费产值。
那么，随着推理效率不断提⾼，如果有⾜够多的⽤⼾持续消耗 Token ， AI 是否已经可以成为⼀⻔
赚钱的⽣意？答案可能⽐想象中乐观，但也更复杂。
⼀张 GPU ，每⼩时 38 美元
DeepSeek 在 9 ⽉ 10 ⽇发布的 V4.1 Flash 中，已经披露了不少推理效率优化的技术细节。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

按照官⽅技术报告，这款模型拥有 5520 亿总参数，采⽤混合专家（ MoE ）架构和新的 Causal
Encoder–Decoder 设计，处理输⼊时约激活 80 亿参数，⽣成输出时约激活 160 亿参数。
模型针对⻓上下⽂和 Agent 场景优化了缓存结构。按照论⽂披露，全局 KV Cache 占⽤降低到上
⼀代的约四分之⼀，⻓期保存的缓存占⽤减少到约⼋分之⼀。每 Token 的全局 KV Cache 约为 890
字节，⽀持最⾼ 100 万 Token 上下⽂。
这些优化直接影响⻓上下⽂推理的显存占⽤、数据传输和服务成本。
10 ⽉ 20 ⽇开源的 DeepInfer ，进⼀步公开了 DeepSeek 如何将这些技术整合为⼤规模在线推理服
务。
它集成了此前陆续开源的 FlashMLA 、 DeepGEMM 、 DeepEP 、 TileKernels 等组件，同时引⼊专
家并⾏、推测解码、异步调度等机制。
图： DeepSeek 在线推理系统⽰意图
DeepSeek 在官⽅部署问答中重点介绍了三项⼯程原则：适配不同⼚商的加速硬件，将 Prefill 与

Decode 分开部署，以及尽可能⻓期保留 KV Cache ，减少重复计算。
在⽣产环境中， Prefill 可以部署在普通 8 卡服务器上，⽽ Decode 采⽤ 64 ⾄ 128 卡超节点，通过⼤
规模专家并⾏提⾼吞吐量。 DeepSeek 还提出将 KV Cache 保留 72 ⼩时以上，以适应 Agent 反复
调⽤相同上下⽂的需求。
这些优化的核⼼⽬标，是在满⾜延迟要求的前提下，让同样数量的 GPU 服务更多请求。
不过，这次开源并不意味着 DeepSeek 已经公开完整⽣产环境中的所有组件。
官⽅ README ⽂档指出， KV Cache 系统、视觉编码与 Prefill/Decode 分离服务编排、跨数据并
⾏副本的负载均衡等，仍需要外部补⻬。部分组件与 DeepSeek 内部基础设施深度耦合，因此尚
未完整开源。
DeepInfer ⽬前也主要⾯向 DeepSeek-V4.1 Flash 。其他模型如果在 MoE 专家组织、注意⼒结
构、数值精度或硬件配置上存在较⼤差异，很难直接照搬。

因此， 38 美元并不代表任何企业下载 DeepInfer 、部署⼀批 GPU ，就能获得同样的收⼊表现。
DeepSeek 也没有完整公开计算这项指标所需的集群硬件构成、成本账单、付费流量⽐例及收⼊
确认⽅式。

### Page 3

但它⾄少说明，在特定模型与硬件条件下，推理⼯程优化能够显著提⾼计算设备的潜在计费产
值。
低价出售 Token ，与⼀张 GPU 产⽣较⾼的计费收⼊，并不⽭盾。
关键在于，⼀⼩时内究竟能够处理多少有效 Token ，以及有多少 Token 真正可以向客⼾收费。
如果推理可以赚钱，为什么模型公司还在亏损？
如果推理效率已经可以做到如此程度，为什么⼀些⼤模型公司仍然需要不断融资，甚⾄持续亏
损？
⼀个重要原因是，训练前沿模型和出售推理服务，本来就是两笔不同的账。
2026 年斯坦福⼤学《 AI Index 》引⽤研究机构 Epoch AI 的数据，估算了 OpenAI 和 Anthropic 在模
型研发与推理服务⽅⾯的计算⽀出。
2025 年， OpenAI 计算⽀出约 163 亿美元，其中研发相关计算⽀出 83 亿美元，推理⽀出 80 亿美
元，两者⼏乎各占⼀半。
2024 年， OpenAI 的相应⽀出分别为 40 亿美元和 18 亿美元。也就是说，⼀年之间，推理计算⽀出
增⻓了约 3.4 倍，⽽研发计算⽀出增⻓约⼀倍。

Anthropic 在 2025 年的计算⽀出估计为 68 亿美元，其中研发相关⽀出 41 亿美元，推理⽀出 27 亿美
元。研发仍然占据较⼤⽐例。
这些数字说明，前沿模型公司的算⼒投⼊结构正在变化。随着商业⽤⼾增加，推理已成为越来
越重要的成本来源，但研发开⽀并没有因为推理业务扩张⽽消失。
⼀家追逐 SOTA 的模型公司，需要同时承担持续训练和⼤规模推理服务的成本。前者包括预训
练、后训练、强化学习、数据处理、模型评测及⼤量实验，后者则需要维持在线服务的算⼒、
容量和稳定性。
Epoch AI 特别指出，研发计算⽀出并不全部⽤于最后⼀次正式训练。⼤量资源实际上消耗在实
验、研究和模型迭代过程中。因此，某个模型公开的单次训练成本，不能简单视为⼀家前沿模
型公司的全部研发成本。
图源： Stanford HAI / Epoch AI ， 2026 年

### Page 4

商业化带来的另⼀层压⼒，是⽤⼾越多，推理成本也可能越⾼。
《 The Information 》今年 2 ⽉报道， OpenAI 在 2025 年的⽑利率约为 33% ，低于 2024 年的 40% ，
也低于此前 46% 的预测。 Anthropic 对 2025 年⽑利率的预期约为 40% ，虽然较 2024 年的负⽑利状
态改善明显，仍⽐其早先⽬标低 10 个百分点。
报道指出，两家公司⽑利率低于预期的重要原因之⼀，是实际推理成本⾼于原先估计。
这与 DeepSeek 的 38 美元看起来形成了⽭盾：⼀边是持续改善的推理效率，另⼀边却是被推理成
本侵蚀的⽑利率。
实际上，它们反映的是同⼀⻔⽣意的两个⽅⾯。
单位 GPU 能够⽣产多少 Token ，是供给效率；公司需要投⼊多少 GPU ，才能满⾜不同客⼾的延
迟、上下⽂⻓度、稳定性和峰值需求，则决定实际经营成本。
即使单位 Token 成本下降，重度⽤⼾增加、任务执⾏时间延⻓、模型调⽤次数上升，也可能使总
推理⽀出迅速增⻓。尤其在固定⽉费的产品中，客⼾⽀付的价格未必随着 Token 消耗同步增加。

想想每天挖空⼼思发 “ 重置卡 ” 的 Tibo 。
图： OpenAI 产品与平台负责⼈ Tibo （ Thibault Sottiaux ）于 10 ⽉ 11 ⽇在 X 平台连续发布有关
Reset 的动态，其中 “Day 6 / Reset” 获得约 290 万次浏览。
⼀家模型公司的推理服务可以拥有正⽑利，整个公司却仍然因为持续研发、基础设施和经营投
⼊⽽亏损。
反过来，如果不承担前沿模型训练，专⻔经营推理服务，⽣意的成本结构就完全不同。
不训练模型，只卖 Token 能不能赚钱？
AI 产业链中已经出现⼀类与前沿模型实验室不同的公司。
它们不以训练最强的基础模型为核⼼业务，⽽是部署 DeepSeek 、 Qwen 、 Llama 等开放权重模
型，通过多芯⽚集群、推理框架、模型优化和调度系统，向开发者与企业销售 API 调⽤、专属推
理实例或私有化部署服务。

### Page 5

对于这些公司来说，模型本⾝未必需要从头研发，成本主要来⾃ GPU 采购或租赁、机房与电
⼒、⽹络和存储、推理系统研发、模型适配，以及销售、运维和客⼾⽀持。
其中最关键的三个变量，是单位 GPU 产值、实际付费利⽤率和综合运营成本。
假设⼀家推理服务商每 GPU ⼩时的完全负担成本为 10 美元，⽽它能够以 DeepSeek 披露的 38 美
元作为满负载计费产值的参考，那么理论上只要实现约 26.3% 的有效计费产出，便可以覆盖这
10 美元成本。
如果有效计费产出达到 50% ，收⼊相当于每⼩时 19 美元，扣除 10 美元后还剩 9 美元。
当然，这只是⽤于说明商业敏感性的假设。实际系统的吞吐量、并发和延迟具有复杂关系，低
负载下的收⼊不⼀定与满负载表现呈线性变化。 10 美元也不是 DeepSeek 披露的真实成本，更不
能将计算结果当作其实际⽑利率。
但这种测算说明，推理系统效率⾜够⾼时，商业回报可能具有较⼤的空间。问题是， GPU 本⾝
并不便宜，购买或者租赁 GPU ，同样不意味着⽴即拥有稳定收⼊。

CoreWeave 提供了⼀个可以对照的真实案例。
2026 年第⼆季度， CoreWeave 收⼊ 25.75 亿美元，同⽐增⻓约 112% ，调整后 EBITDA 达到 15.10
亿美元，利润率为 59% 。
但在美国通⽤会计准则下，公司当季仍净亏损 6.26 亿美元。问题出在算⼒⽣意⾼昂的资产和资⾦
成本上。 CoreWeave 需要投⼊⼤量资⾦采购 GPU 及配套设备，相关资产随着使⽤逐年折旧；同
时，公司依靠债务融资⽀持扩张，还要承担⾼额利息。 2026 年第⼆季度，其折旧及摊销费⽤达
到 13.93 亿美元，净利息⽀出⾼达 6.4 亿美元。
折旧反映的是已经购⼊的算⼒设备在使⽤期间逐步计⼊的成本，利息是为这些投资融资所付出
的代价。即使 GPU 租赁带来了可观收⼊，这两项负担仍可能⼤幅压缩利润。
CoreWeave 的财报也说明，算⼒利⽤率⾼、收⼊增⻓快，并不意味着经营算⼒就⼀定赚钱。
更复杂的是，推理算⼒并不是⼀个完全同质化的市场。
DeepSeek 能够通过深度优化获得较⾼单位 GPU 产值，不意味着普通推理服务商可以⽤相同的硬

件、相同的价格获得同样表现。
因此，推理服务可能是⼀⻔具有可观⽑利空间的⽣意，但它并不会⾃动成为⾼利润⽣意。⼯程
能⼒影响成本下限，客⼾需求和定价权决定收⼊上限，⽽资本成本影响最终能留下多少利润。
此外，推理市场还⾯临持续降价的压⼒。
DeepSeek 降低单位 Token 成本之后，也在通过更低的 API 价格将⼀部分效率收益传递给⽤⼾。
其他公司⼀旦采⽤更⾼效的算法和系统，也可能参与价格竞争。
推理效率提升创造的收益，最终未必全部留给卖 Token 的公司。
AI 利润版图，谁能赚钱？

### Page 6

从英伟达、台积电、 SK 海⼒⼠，到数据中⼼和云服务商， AI 基础设施投资已经为部分上游企业
带来了可观的收⼊和利润。
但对于经营推理服务的企业来说，设备买回来之后，仍然需要不断寻找客⼾和任务，才能持续
创造收⼊。
⽬前， Coding 是商业化最清晰的 AI 应⽤市场之⼀，但即使在这个付费意愿较强的领域，盈利也
并不容易。
《 The Information 》披露， Cursor 在截⾄ 2026 年 1 ⽉的季度中，⽑利率⼀度为 -23% ；如果将免
费⽤⼾的推理费⽤全部计⼊销售成本，⽑利率约为 -31% 。虽然此后⽑利率已经转正，但⾼昂的
模型调⽤成本，仍是 AI 应⽤需要⾯对的问题。
消费应⽤的情况更加分化。 10 ⽉ 12 ⽇，曾经的明星 AI 陪伴公司 Character.AI 申请破产保护。根据
破产⽂件及公开报道，公司当时仍拥有约 1200 万⽉活⽤⼾， 2026 年 8 ⽉收⼊达到 530 万美元，但
每⽉经营亏损仍有 800 万⾄ 900 万美元。
仅 2026 年前⼋个⽉， Character.AI 就花费了 5610 万美元购买算⼒，占运营费⽤约 45% 。即使转

向更便宜的 GPU 和开放权重模型，并削减⼈员开⽀，也未能实现盈利。法律诉讼和监管压⼒同
样加剧了公司的困境。
但是， AI 原⽣应⽤并⾮⽆法盈利。 Midjourney 创始⼈ David Holz 在今年 3 ⽉接受《 The
Information 》采访时表⽰，公司 2023 年收⼊已显著超过 2 亿美元，此后继续增⻓，并且保持盈
利。
但它也不是所有 AI 应⽤的普遍样本。图像⽣成拥有相对明确的使⽤场景和付费价值，⽽全天候运
⾏的个⼈ Agent ，可能需要持续调⽤模型、使⽤⼯具和重新规划任务，成本结构更加复杂。
从整体市场看，需求实际上正在快速增⻓。
Menlo Ventures 在 2026 年 9 ⽉发布的《 State of Consumer AI 》报告估计，全球消费者 AI ⽀出已
达到 400 亿美元，是 2025 年 120 亿美元的三倍多。
但值得注意的是，美国成年⼈ AI 使⽤率只从 2025 年的 61% 上升到 64% 。消费者 AI ⽀出的⾼速增
⻓，主要来⾃已有⽤⼾使⽤得更深⼊，付费更多。新增⽤⼾有限。

图： Menlo Ventures 消费 AI ⽀出图

### Page 7

Menlo Ventures 合伙⼈ Amy Wu Martin 指出，今年令⼈意外的变化，是消费者 AI 市场在没有迎来
⼤量新⽤⼾的情况下实现了三倍增⻓。推动增⻓的，是重度⽤⼾将 AI 带⼊更多⽣活场景，并开始
允许 AI 代表⾃⼰采取⾏动。
现在，更重要的是⼀个⽤⼾每天有多少任务交给 AI ，以及每项任务能够创造多少实际价值。这也
是 Personal Agent （ PA ）重新成为⼤⼚和创业公司共同关注⽅向的原因之⼀。
最近推出的 Duet 、 Dots 、 Muse 、 Grokbot 以及⼀批独⽴创业公司的产品，虽然在⼊⼝、记忆、
权限体系和执⾏⽅式上存在不同，但都在尝试扩⼤ AI 能够承接的个⼈任务范围。
如果 Agent 能够持续处理邮件、安排⼯作、操作软件、完成采购、协调不同服务， AI 调⽤便可能
从⽤⼾主动发起的短时会话，转向围绕实际任务展开的⻓流程执⾏。
Agent 之间的协作，以及越来越多由机器直接发起的⼯具调⽤和服务请求，也可能进⼀步扩⼤推
理消费市场。
不过，更多 Token 消耗本⾝并不代表商业成功。

⼀家 PA 公司如果每⽉向⽤⼾收取 20 美元，却需要花费 30 美元购买模型调⽤和其他服务，那么它
可能帮助上游模型商扩⼤收⼊，⾃⼰却仍然⽆法盈利。
同样，模型越来越⾼效，也可能让过去需要⼤量 Token 的任务变得便宜。即使 Agent 数量⼤幅增
⻓，市场总收⼊也不⼀定以相同⽐例增⻓。
模型公司希望扩⼤推理消费市场，应⽤公司希望以更低的推理成本完成任务，⽤⼾关⼼实际完
成的⼯作值多少钱。
DeepSeek 的 38 美元，展⽰了在特定条件下，⾼效率⽣产 Token 所具有的潜在收⼊能⼒。
过去⼏年，资本市场为 AI 投⼊了巨额资⾦，芯⽚、存储和算⼒基础设施率先从中受益。随着推理
系统继续进步，模型公司和应⽤公司⼀直在⾯对另外⼀道更复杂的商业命题：如果越来越多企
业都能以更低的成本⽣产智能，谁能找到⾜够多愿意购买智能的客⼾？
还有更难的问题：如果 AI 是⼀个新的时代， token 承载的 “ 智能 ” ，要创造什么增量？ AI Native 的
核⼼逻辑，还在⼀⽚混沌中等待。
本⽂来源：腾讯科技

### Page 8

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

삼성전자 (005930)

Company Brief 
반도체/디스플레이 
Analyst 김선우 
sunwoo.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 우서현 
seohyun.woo@meritz.co.kr 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정  
3Q26E 영업이익 106.1조원, 원화강세 불구 견조한 수준 기록 전망 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익은 106.1조원으로 낮아진 시장 기대치 (104조원)를 소폭 
능가할 전망이다. 해당 분기동안 급격한 원화강세로 인해 실적 측면의 비우호적 환경
이 조성되었지만, 동사는 강건한 판매 정책에 기반해 메모리 판가가 산업 평균을 재
차 능가하며 견조한 실적 개선이 가능했다. 
동사 메모리 사업부의 3Q26 영업이익은 109조원으로 예상되며, 이중 DRAM과 
NAND는 각각 80조원 (OPM 78.5%) 과 29조원 (OPM 68.9%) 을 차지할 전망이다. 
DRAM과 NAND 출하량은 기존 예상치를 소폭 하회하는 +3.4%/+4.2% QoQ을 기
록하겠지만, 판가가 무려 +20.1%/19.0% QoQ 상승하며 실적 개선을 이끌겠다. 전
분기에 이어 ‘DS 특별경영성과급’ 충당금이 반영되며 손익에 일부 영향을 미치겠지
만, 동사 특유의 ‘수익성 극대화’ 판가 정책이 산업 우위 구도를 조성한다. 한편 LSI/
파운드리는 영업손실이 1.5조원을 기록하며 적자가 지속될 전망이다. 가동률 개선에 
기반한 사업 회복이 진행중이지만, 테일러팹 가동과 함께 비용 요인이 지속 출회할 
전망이다. 한편 반도체 외 기타 사업부의 험난한 실적 추이는 지속될 전망이다. SDC 
1.1조원 영업이익을 제외하고, DX (세트) 부문의 경우 MX -1.9조원, DA/VD -7,400
억원으로 영업적자 확대되리라 예상된다. 당사가 지난해 말부터 주장했듯, 세트 사업
은 메모리 등 부품원가 비중이 급격히 상승하는 가운데 향후 ‘판가 인상 → 판매량 
축소’의 ‘내구재화’ 변모 속 적자 최소화 노력만이 불가피할 전망이다.

1Q27 일부 모바일 DRAM 판가 +50% 폭등 예상. 적극적 매수 권고 
AGI를 향한 경쟁적이며 구조적 서버 수요 증가 불구, 스마트폰 중심의 B2C 응용처
는 유일한 판가저항처로 남아있다. 하지만 당사의 채널체크에 따르면, 메모리 업체들
의 지속적인 생산 전환 (LPDDR → 서버 DRAM 및 HBM)에 기반해 주요 고객 향 
모바일 메모리 판가는 무려 1Q27부터 50% 이상 QoQ 상향될 전망이다. 주요 고객
은 내년 20주년 플래그십 스마트폰을 제작하는 상황에서 판가 폭등을 감내해야하는 
상황이다. 최근 메모리 사이클 개선 속도에 대한 막연하며 진부한 우려가 존재하나, 
향후 1~2개월간 메모리 업체들에 대한 2027년 실적 추정치 상향과 함께 ‘성장·저평
가·주주환원 매력’이 동시에 발생할 전망이다. 동사의 2027년 영업이익 추정치를 기
존의 562조원에서 632조원으로 13% 상향조정하며, 시장 눈높이 역시 지속 상향되
리라 예상한다. 한편 배당 중심으로 발표됐던 동사의 주주환원의 경우, 모든 주주에게 
더 실효적인 ‘자사주 매입·소각’ 방안이 병행되는 방향으로 변모하리라 예상된다. 투
자의견 Buy와 적정주가 50만원을 유지한다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 7

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 500,000원 
현재주가 (10.6) 272,000원 
상승여력 83.8%

Document 200: AIがサイバー攻撃を自動化する時代、サイバー株は上がるのか ～攻撃能力の急伸と企業業績・株価から検証する～

Source PDF: all report pdf/AIがサイバー攻撃を自動化する時代、サイバー株は上がるのか ～攻撃能力の急伸と企業業績・株価から検証する～.pdf

### Page 1

Research Report

2026 年 10 月 6 日

1/6 
AI がサイバー攻撃を自動化する時代、サイバー株
は上がるのか 
～攻撃能力の急伸と企業業績・株価から検証する～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  柏村 祐

（要旨） 
○ AI によるサイバー攻撃の自動化・高度化が現実のものとなっている。豪政府システムへのAI
侵入事件などが示すように、AI は与えられた目的を達成する過程で、許可された範囲を超え
てシステムにアクセスしうることが明らかになった。国内でも 9 月下旬以降、幅広い業種の
企業で不正アクセスの公表が相次いでいる。 
○ AI エージェントのサイバー課題解決能力はここ数年で急速に向上しており、サイバーセキュ
リティ企業の売上高・利益も足元で好調に推移している。 
○ 一方で、高度な AI モデルの登場は既存のサイバーセキュリティ製品の陳腐化リスクへの懸
念を生み、関連株が下落するというパラドックスも起きている。「サイバー株なら何でも上
がる」という安易な連想買いには注意が必要である。 
○ 個人投資家は、サイバーセキュリティセクター全体を丸ごと買うのではなく、「AIネイティ
ブ」なサイバーセキュリティ企業や、AIを活用した防衛技術を持つ巨大テック企業（メガテ
ック）への選別投資が求められる。

1. 豪政府システムへの「AI侵入事件」が鳴らす警鐘 
2026 年9月、オーストラリアのアルバニージー首相は、同国の公的医療保険制度（Medicare）
の統計ポータルサイトに、OpenAIのAIエージェントが無断で侵入していたと発表した。注目すべ
きは、これが悪意あるハッカーによるサイバー攻撃ではなく、OpenAIの研究チームがAIに調査を
実行させる過程で起きた点だ。AIは調査中にアクセス制限に直面すると、自ら制限を回避する方
法を見つけ出し、非公開のファイルにまでアクセスしたという。この事例は、AIが与えられた目
的を達成する過程で、許可された範囲を超えてシステムにアクセスしうることを示した。 
サイバー攻撃の脅威は、海の向こうだけの話ではない。日本でも9月下旬以降、物流、交通、
小売、カーシェアリングなど幅広い業種の企業が、不正アクセスによる個人情報流出の可能性を
相次いで公表している。これらの攻撃にAIが用いられたかどうかは明らかになっていないが、サ
イバー攻撃の影響が業種を問わず及んでいることは確かだ。政府も10月1 日から、能動的サイバ

### Page 2

2/6 
ー防御の一環として、重大なサイバー攻撃を未然に防ぐため、警察や自衛隊が攻撃元のサーバー
等にアクセスし、無害化措置を講じることを可能にした。 
AIがサイバー攻撃を自動化・高度化させる時代が到来しつつある。企業や政府の防御需要は急
増し、サイバーセキュリティ企業には特需がもたらされると期待される。個人投資家の間の「AI
の次なる恩恵銘柄はサイバー株」という見方にも、一定の説得力がある。しかし、 「サイバー株な
ら何でも上がる」と考えるのは早計である。AIによる攻撃の自動化・高度化は、攻撃件数を増や
すだけでなく、従来のセキュリティ製品や防御手法の有効性そのものを揺るがしかねないから
だ。本稿では、AIの攻撃能力の現在地と、それがサイバーセキュリティ企業の業績および株価に
与える影響を、データに基づいて検証する。そのうえで、 「サイバー株＝安全確実な成長株」とい
う見方をどう見直すべきか、個人投資家に向けて投資戦略を考えるための新たな視点を提示した
い。

2. AIの攻撃能力急伸とサイバーセキュリティ企業の業績・株価への影響 
(1) AIは人間なしでどこまで攻撃できるのか 
AIエージェントのサイバー攻撃関連能力は急速に高まっている。スタンフォード大学人間中心
AI研究所（Stanford HAI）が公表した「AI Index Report 2026」によれば、AIは人間が逐次指示
を与えなくても、専門的なサイバー課題を自律的に解ける水準に達しつつある。 
同レポートで紹介されている「Cybench」は、暗号、Webセキュリティ、リバースエンジニアリ
ング、フォレンジック、エクスプロイトなど、40の専門レベルのサイバーセキュリティ課題を評
価するベンチマークである。資料1 は、人間のガイドなし（unguided）でAIが課題を解決できた
割合の推移を示している。これを見ると、2024年時点ではわずか15％であった解決率が、2025年
には 82％へと急伸し、2026年には93％に達している。2年間で 78ポイントの上昇であり、AIが
自律的にサイバー課題を解く能力が大きく向上していることがわかる。

### Page 3

3/6 
資料 1 AI エージェントの自律的なサイバー課題解決能力は急伸している 
（注）サイバーセキュリティのベンチマーク「Cybench」における、人間のガイドなし（unguided）での課題解決率（％）の推移。
Cybench は暗号、Web セキュリティ、リバースエンジニアリング、フォレンジック、エクスプロイトなど 40 の専門レベル課題を評価
するもの。 
（出所）Stanford HAI, “AI Index Report 2026”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

(2) サイバー需要は企業の売上・利益に届いているのか 
こうしたAIによるサイバー攻撃の脅威が高まるなか、防御側であるサイバーセキュリティ企業
への需要拡大が期待される。実際、その需要はすでに企業業績に表れている。 
Nasdaqが公表した「Q1 2026 HXRXL Cybersecurity Commentary」によれば、サイバーセキュリ
ティ関連銘柄で構成される「Nasdaq ISE Cyber Security Select Index（HXRXL） 」構成企業の
2026 年第1 四半期決算は極めて好調であった。同レポートによると、構成企業の売上高は前年同
期比で 11％増加し、純利益は同29.3％の増加を記録しており、売上高以上に純利益が大きく伸び
ていることが確認できる。さらに、市場予想に対する結果をまとめた資料2を見ても、売上高で
予想を上回った企業は指数ウェイトの92.2％を占め、純利益に至っては100％の企業が予想を上
回った。サイバーセキュリティ需要の強さは、売上高と利益の双方に表れている。

### Page 4

4/6 
資料 2 サイバーセキュリティ企業の業績は市場予想を大きく上回る 
（注）Nasdaq ISE Cyber Security Select Index（HXRXL）構成企業の 2026 年第 1 四半期決算の集計結果。市場予想に対する売上
高および純利益の予想超え・予想未達の企業数（指数ウェイトベース）、ならびに平均上振れ・下振れ率（％）を示す。 
（出所）Nasdaq, “Q1 2026 HXRXL Cybersecurity Commentary”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

(3) それでもサイバー株は上がるのか 
業績が好調であれば株価も上昇すると考えるのが自然だが、 「AIによる攻撃高度化＝サイバー株
上昇」とは必ずしも言い切れない。AIは防御需要を増やす一方で、既存のサイバーセキュリティ
製品を陳腐化させるとの懸念も生んでいるからだ。 
国際決済銀行（BIS）が2026 年7月に公表したレポート「A Mythos moment? Frontier AI and 
cyber risk」は、このパラドックスを指摘している。2026年 4月にAnthropicが高度なサイバー攻
撃能力を持つAIモデル「Mythos」を発表した際、サイバーセキュリティ関連株は大きく下落し
た。資料3 は、Mythos発表前を0とした場合のサイバーセキュリティ関連株の累積異常リターン
（CAR）を示している。発表後、サイバーセキュリティETF（IHAK）や主要企業（CRWD、PANWな
ど）のCARは軒並みマイナスに転じ、一部の系列ではグラフ上で約−30％の大幅なマイナスを記録
した。BISは、この市場反応について「新しいAIモデルの登場により、既存のサイバーセキュリテ
ィ製品やサービスが陳腐化する（obsolescence）という投資家の懸念を反映している」との解釈
を紹介している。つまり、AIの進化はサイバーセキュリティ市場全体を拡大させる可能性が高い
ものの、同時にゲームチェンジを引き起こし、旧来の技術やサービスに依存する企業の競争力を
急速に奪うリスクを孕んでいる。

### Page 5

5/6 
資料 3 高度な AI モデルの発表後、サイバーセキュリティ関連株は下落した 
（注）2026 年 4 月の高度な AI モデル「Mythos」発表前後の、サイバーセキュリティ関連株の累積異常リターン（CAR、％）。発表前
を 0 として推移を示している。「Glasswing partners」は CrowdStrike（CRWD）および Palo Alto Networks（PANW）を指す。 
（出所）Bank for International Settlements, “BIS Bulletin No. 129: A Mythos moment? Frontier AI and cyber risk”をもとに第一ラ
イフ資産運用経済研究所作成

3. AI時代のサイバー株投資～「陳腐化リスク」を回避する新戦略～ 
第 2 章で見てきたように、AI はサイバー攻撃能力を飛躍的に高めている。また、足元のサイバ
ーセキュリティ企業の業績も好調である。しかし、最先端のAIモデルが登場した際には、市場は
既存のサイバーセキュリティ製品の「陳腐化リスク」に敏感に反応し、関連株は大きく売り込ま
れた。ここからいえるのは、サイバーセキュリティ市場全体は今後も拡大が見込まれるものの、
企業間の「勝ち負け」はこれまで以上に鮮明になるということだ。こうした環境変化のなか、個
人投資家には従来の投資アプローチを根本から見直すことが求められる。 
第一に、サイバーセキュリティセクター全体を丸ごと買うアプローチ（テーマ型ETFやインデ
ックスファンドなど）には注意が必要である。市場全体が成長しても、AIの進化に取り残され、
陳腐化して淘汰される旧世代の企業をポートフォリオに抱え込むリスクが高まるからだ。足元の
業績や過去の市場シェアだけで安心することはできない。第二に、個別銘柄を選別する際は、 「AI
ネイティブ」なサイバーセキュリティ企業であるかどうかが最大の焦点となる。攻撃側がAI を駆
使する以上、防御側もAIをシステムの中心に据え、未知の脅威を自律的に検知・遮断できるプラ
ットフォームを持っていなければ対抗できない。レガシーな製品を販売している企業ではなく、
クラウドベースで膨大なデータをAI で解析し、防御機能を継続的に高度化できる企業を見極める
必要がある。第三に、サイバー防衛の投資対象を「セキュリティ専業企業」以外にも広げること
である。 
最先端のAI モデルを開発し、強固なクラウドインフラを持つ巨大テック企業（メガテック）
は、自社のAI を活用した防衛技術を、顧客向けの強力なセキュリティサービスとして提供し始め

### Page 6

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。

ている。豊富な資金力とAI 開発力を持つメガテックを、サイバー防衛の担い手として評価し、ポ
ートフォリオの中核に据えることも、AI時代の投資戦略として有効な選択肢となるだろう。

2 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The U.S. Capex Boom No One is Talking About—And Its Implications
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
We have long been of the belief that the U.S. is on the cusp of an inbound FDI capex boom
that can result in numerous positive spillover effects for the U.S. economy. The latest

figures from the U.S. Department of Commerce bear this out.
Exhibit 1 highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be inside the U.S.,
with FDI totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year. That is a staggering
103% jump from a year ago and places FDI inflows on track for a record year.
The latest inbound capex already adds to the massive scale of foreign investment in the
U.S. To wit, the accumulated stock of FDI in the U.S. stands at nearly $6T, based on the

latest figures from the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA). Manufacturing accounts for
roughly 43% of the total, and, at last count, some 10,000 foreign affiliates were operating
in the U.S.
These firms are largely invisible to the average U.S. investor, but the activities of U.S.
affiliates of foreign multinationals are substantial and competitive- and growth-enhancing.
To this point and based on the latest data from the BEA, foreign affiliates in the U.S.

employed nearly 8.6 million U.S. workers in 2024. That represents 6.1% of total private
sector employment, with manufacturing and retail the two most prominent sectors
of employment. Roughly 24% of all manufacturing jobs in the U.S. are from foreign
subsidiaries. In terms of pay, U.S. workers at these firms make $89,000 annually, 7% higher
than the national average, according to the Global Business Alliance.
Foreign affiliates also contributed some $1.5T to U.S. GDP; comprised some 15.7% of total

U.S. private sector investment; accounted for 12.4% of total U.S. research & development
(R&D) expenditures; paid roughly 20% of all U.S. federal taxes; and made up 22.5% of
U.S. exports and 26.2% of U.S. imports. 1 These are the direct effects of U.S. subsidiaries
of foreign companies operating in the U.S. The indirect effects are even greater in terms
of more income, jobs, growth and tax revenue—all bolstering the long-term competitive
advantage of the U.S. In a nutshell, foreign firms operating in the U.S. are an important

ingredient of U.S. economic growth.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Companies that once served the U.S. market exclusively through exports
increasingly face higher costs and more uncertainties and hence want to be “inside.”
Two, with a $32T-plus economy with extraordinary consumer purchasing power, foreign

firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment than just trade (exports). There
is no more dynamic consumer in the world than the American shopper.
Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers, semiconductors, power
generation, etc., has created numerous investment opportunities for many foreign firms
that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging capex cycle.
Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly developed

technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.
As more U.S. affiliates of foreign multinationals set up shop in the U.S., think more job
creation across the country, more manufacturing output, more locally sourced purchases,
more U.S. exports, more spending on R&D, and more tax revenue for local communities.
And over the long run, think a more globally competitive U.S. economy.
1 Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign Multinational Enterprises, 2024” and “Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign

Multinational Enterprises, 2023”, BEA July 2026.
Investment Implications
The boom in foreign investment in
the U.S. should continue to support
employment, productivity, corporate
earnings, and broader U.S. economic
competitiveness, in our view. Hence
our U.S. bias.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
And speaking of global competitiveness, Exhibit 2A is very illustrative of the magnet pull of
the U.S. economy to attract FDI versus the rest of the world. Of new greenfield investment
this decade, note the overriding preference for the U.S., accounting for nearly 16% of total
global greenfield investment this decade. America’s share is larger than the next three
countries combined—the United Kingdom, India, and Germany. And note that America’s

share of new investment is more than 5X that of China’s. Most of this new investment
has gone into manufacturing sectors like electrical equipment, computers, appliances and
batteries and has originated from Asia and Europe.
The bulk of foreign investment comes from Europe, followed by Japan and Canada, but as
Exhibit 2B highlights, there is a new dynamic playing out when it comes to America’s ability
to attract capital. There is a new source in town: a handful of developing nations (namely

Taiwan, South Korea, Mexico and Brazil). Mexican manufacturers, Taiwan technology
leaders, South Korea chip leaders, Brazil industrial firms, India pharmaceutical firms—these
companies and more are realizing that in today’s trade environment, the best way to serve
the American market is not necessarily through exports but through investment—investing
locally. FDI flows from developing nations reached $63B in the 12 months through June,
more than double the level a year prior ($24.5B), according to the BEA. Fueled by insatiable

AI demand, flows from South Korea alone totaled $23.4B in the first half of 2026. The
upshot is that an increasingly important source of capital for the U.S. is coming from an
unlikely source: the developing nations.
More fire for the capex boom. The coming boom in FDI inflows is supportive of our thesis
that the U.S. stands on the cusp of a major industrial upgrade. We are in the early innings
of a structural—not cyclical—capex cycle that should lead to the rebuilding of domestic

supply chains, higher value-added manufacturing, and more AI-driven productivity growth.
Foreign firms, in our opinion, will be part of this process.
That is important to recognize because remember the following: when a foreign company
builds a factory or an R&D facility or expands its existing manufacturing capacity right
here in the U.S., good things happen. Demand for U.S. goods and services goes up (as does
demand for construction, materials, logistics and professional services). New jobs are created,

boosting incomes of U.S. workers, while supporting more local spending and consumption.
And because many foreign firms often bring advanced manufacturing technologies when
investing in the U.S., the end results include a boost in productivity and innovation in various
sectors of the economy.
All the above, of course, are the secret ingredients to stronger U.S. economic growth, rising
corporate earnings and higher equity prices. With a little help from our friends, America’s

industrial renewal is gaining strength and momentum.

MARKET VIEW
Europe’s Electoral Cycle and Market Risks
Ehiwario Efeyini, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
As midterm elections approach for the U.S. next month, voters across Europe will also
enter their own election season starting this November and continuing throughout
2027. Over the next year, 52% of the total EU population, representing 46% of EU GDP
across eight countries, will go to the polls, including voters in three of its four largest

economies (Exhibit 3). Having announced snap general elections earlier this month,
Spain will vote for members of its lower and upper house on November 29. A series of
parliamentary elections are then due for 2027 in Estonia (by March 7), Finland (on April
18), Greece (by July 25), Slovakia (by September 28), Poland (by November 11) and Italy
(by December 22). And perhaps most consequentially France will stage Presidential
elections on April 18, with a potential second round of voting set for May 2.

For the EU as a whole, the significance of these coming elections will be the potential
for a shift toward more populist, domestically oriented policy priorities from leading
opposition candidates (particularly in France, Spain and Poland) which could obstruct the
process of closer EU integration. In the nearer term, adoption of the new seven-year
(2028-2034) EU budget due by the end of 2027 could be impeded. And over the longer
term, renewed efforts toward an EU-wide capital market union to deepen and attract

more inward investment into European markets could also be further delayed.
At the country level, investors have seen bond yields rise globally in 2026 on the back of
higher inflation and concerns over fiscal sustainability. The debt question has arguably
been most pressing in parts of Europe, especially given the likelihood that upcoming
elections make for domestic increases in government largesse. And of the eight
countries going to the polls over the next year, France remains the biggest source of

concern. Interest rate spreads on French 10-year sovereign bonds have overtaken those
in both Greece and Italy this year (Exhibit 4), driven by investor concerns over public
debt. The 2027 government budget unveiled earlier this month projected a deficit of
5.0% of GDP (well in excess of the 3% specified in the EU Stability and Growth Pact),
but remained insufficiently expansive to nullify the risk that the current government
could be unseated later this year if it is forced through the fragmented parliament. This

comes on top of an existing debt load of close to 120% of GDP and a halving of official
2026 growth forecasts from 1.0% at the start of the year to 0.5% in September. In
short, all four variables in France’s debt sustainability equation currently stand as major
headwinds, and the two leading candidates in the 2027 election have shown limited
appetite for fiscal consolidation over the coming years.

We remain cautious on European
markets overall, despite seeing

areas such as defense, data center

On top of growing uncertainty
from the upcoming electoral cycle

and interest rate outlook, rising
competition and slowing demand
from China continue to challenge
key segments of the Consumer

direct AI concentration also leaves
Europe under-exposed to the key
growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and
emerging Asia.

Intervention from the ECB under its 2022 bond purchase program is also unlikely at this

stage given that the spread widening in France has not appeared either disorderly or
unwarranted. Meanwhile, conditions could potentially be aggravated by expected ECB rate
hikes in response to still above-target inflation across the eurozone. Indeed recent moves
to tighten monetary policy from a number of major central banks have been read most
hawkishly in Europe. Most of the increase in European bond yields has come from higher
inflation breakevens as opposed to real yields in the U.S. and Japan. And despite some

paring back of tightening expectations in the wake of recent bond volatility, the market is
still pricing three more policy rate increases over the next 12 months.
Euro depreciation and an emerging shift toward more protectionist trade policy with China
could further complicate the ECB policy outlook. China posted a record $35B trade surplus,
according to Eurostat, with the EU in September (alongside its $938B total surplus for the
first three quarters of 2026), and the two markets have agreed to reduce the EU bilateral

deficit by capping imports of hybrid cars for four years. Hybrids account for less than 2%
of total EU imports from China, but this could potentially mark the start of a broader shift
toward more trade protection in Europe with its largest foreign supplier which risks adding
further upward pressure to cost-push inflation. And though the leading candidates in the
French election may favor resuming oil and gas imports from Russia, Europe also remains

among the most at risk from the global energy shock. Indeed some 56% of the region’s
crude oil consumption is in the form of diesel product according to the International Energy
Agency, more than twice the U.S. share.
The 2- to 10-year eurozone yield curve has already flattened significantly since the start
of the year. And at just over 30 basis points as of mid-October, it remains flatter than
its equivalents in the U.S., UK and Japan. Outside Energy and Technology, Financials have

been among the strongest-performing sectors in Europe this year. But additional ECB
rate hikes could lead to further curve flattening, compressing bank net interest margins
and contributing to slower growth—particularly given that rate increases are coming in
response to supply-driven inflation rather than reflecting stronger economic activity.
We nonetheless continue to see pockets of strength in European markets. Growing
defense budgets should remain a tailwind for Industrials as national governments look to

meet their North Atlantic Treaty Organization targets over the next several years. And the
sector should be further supported by indirect exposure to AI investment through electric
cabling, power infrastructure and building materials for data centers. Leaders in renewable
energy equipment and services are also likely to be beneficiaries of grid upgrades and
broadening electrification globally, particularly given elevated fossil fuel prices. But our view

on the region remains cautious overall. On top of growing uncertainty from the upcoming
electoral cycle and interest rate outlook, rising competition and slowing demand from
China continue to challenge key segments of Europe’s Consumer Discretionary sector.
Limited direct AI concentration in technology hardware, cloud services and application
software also leaves Europe underexposed to the key growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and emerging Asia.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Cautious Optimism Entering Fiscal Year 2027
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
“A renewed focus on rebuilding fiscal space is urgent, given elevated debt, higher
borrowing costs, multiplying demands on public budgets and heightened uncertainty.” So
warned the IMF about the global fiscal outlook last week in its updated World Economic

Outlook report. Much is at stake for developed nations in Europe and Asia facing high
existing debt, unfavorable demographics and more modest growth.
For the U.S., between structurally higher mandatory outlays, the administration’s $1.5T
proposed defense package, and the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield sitting above 5%, spending
is likely headed higher. We continue to fade concerns of an impending U.S. debt crisis,
however, largely because economic growth continues to surprise to the upside. Expanding

output is good news for the debt-to-GDP ratio, which remains a touch below 100%.
Indeed, rather than being led higher on account of un-anchored inflation expectations, the
10-year U.S. Treasury yield has repriced to reflect better-than-expected nominal growth
(Exhibit 5A). Higher yields in the face of slowing growth, meanwhile, would be a different
story, not only for the fiscal outlook (worse) but also for U.S. equity valuations (lower).
Of course, after the U.S. accumulated another $2T deficit in FY 2026, 2 “rebuilding fiscal

space” in the IMF’s words will be politically unpopular and mathematically challenging.
In our view, the biggest near-term tail risk would be a surprise move lower in growth as
budget deficits at 6% of GDP in good times leave little room for bad times. This isn’t our
base case given robust consumer spending per our internal credit and debit card data and
ongoing investment in the AI buildout and beyond. We expect these two pillars to sustain
growth in 2027.

The other good news is that, contrary to concerns, demand for Treasurys remains robust.
Yields are attractive—that was the message from October’s successful $39B 10-year
Treasury note auction. At the same time, U.S. debt boasts an array of buyers: domestic
households, the Federal Reserve, mutual funds and exchange-traded funds, money market
funds, pension funds, foreign central banks, private investors overseas, and more. Per
Exhibit 5B, whereas foreign official investors once dominated some 40% of the U.S.

Treasury market during the 2008/2009 Great Financial Crisis, their share has fallen to
12% while domestic private investors now account for a majority of holdings. Translated:
demand is more diverse than most appreciate.

for investors seeking income and can

rate volatility. We expect demand for
U.S. assets to remain healthy and favor
the U.S. over developed economies in

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: managing expectations
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
11:04 October 23 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
With little domestic data of significance, participants’ attention focused on RBA Deputy Governor
Hauser's fireside chat, which saw an abrupt shift in market expectations of a follow-up rate hike at the
November meeting.
Hauser warned in his remarks that the current inflation outlook was "materially worse" than the RBA's

### Page 2

August forecasts. Three upside inflation risks were characterised as having "crystallised" ahead of the
September meeting – the Middle East, the AI boom, and domestic supply constraints. Hauser noted the
Middle East picture "looks worse" given oil prices are "about a third higher than [the RBA] assumed in
August”. He then positioned AI and data centre developments among an "extraordinary sequence of
positive demand shocks around the world", with "higher AI prices, tech prices feeding through into

CPI". Finally, on domestic capacity pressures, the Deputy Governor noted that "inflation's been coming
in at or a little bit above expectations, despite an apparent easing in the labour market". He went on to
state that the staff are currently assessing "whether [the RBA] need to change [its] judgement on
capacity pressures in the labour market again" – in other words, revise up their assumption for the
NAIRU.
Hauser's comments prompted a material reassessment of market pricing for a follow-up November

hike, from just a 20% chance at the end of last week to a 50/50 proposition today ahead of next week's
Q3 CPI. For that release, we are forecasting a 1.3% (4.1%yr) lift in headline inflation and a 0.9 (3.5%yr)
increase in trimmed mean inflation. Our preview provides more detail behind the component mix for Q3
as well as our upward revisions to the preliminary Q4 forecast. Provided inflation prints broadly in line
with our expectations, the RBA is likely to hike by 25bp at the November meeting.

Across in New Zealand, consumer prices rose 0.7% in Q3 and annual inflation eased from 4.1%yr to
3.7%yr. Under the surface of this ‘as expected’ read though, several closely watched measures of core
inflation took a step higher to now be at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s target band. Our NZ
economics team consequently expect the RBNZ will raise the OCR 25bps to 3.00% at its October
policy review, while retaining a 4.00% terminal OCR forecast for September 2027.

Earlier in the week, China's economy surprised to the upside in Q3, GDP growth accelerating from
0.9%qtr to 1.2%qtr, lifting annual growth from 4.3%yr to 4.5%yr – keeping authorities on track to
achieve their 4.5-5.0% full-year growth objective.
While annual retail sales growth improved in September, rising from 0.4%yr to 1.1%yr, the current pace
of growth is only a quarter of the gain seen just a year ago and very weak versus history. Consumer
spending continues to be constrained by weak confidence, particularly with respect to employment

prospects and wealth. On housing wealth, five years into the property downturn, house prices continue
to decline month-on-month and investment remains in a deep downturn, -20.6%ytd.
Total fixed asset investment has been detracting from annual GDP growth since April owing to a lull in
high-tech investment, a lack of confidence in future demand amongst domestically oriented businesses
as well as property’s malaise. For aggregate growth to hold up through 2027 and 2028 as the

contribution from net exports moderates, broad-based stimulus in scale will prove necessary. Expect
recent targeted support measures to be broadened and given much greater scale in coming months.
Finally, in the UK, inflation accelerated from 3.1%yr to 3.5%yr in September, exceeding both market
expectations and the Bank of England's July forecast. The surprise was largely driven by energy, fuel
prices rising 5.4%mth. However, core inflation also edged up from 2.6%yr to 2.8%yr as services

inflation increased from 3.4%yr to 3.7%yr. While not an immediate concern inflation, the labour market
showing resilience, the ILO unemployment rate falling from 4.9% to 4.7% in August, suggests demand

### Page 3

Related articles
risks for inflation will need close monitoring as well as supply-side concerns.
Next week will see the focus once again return to the US as the FOMC meet and US Q3 GDP is
released. For both events, the implications for risks to the outlook will be the focus. The week after, the
US mid-term elections are due.

Document 264: Entering the age of the older consumer As America ages, older consumers have more leisure time, significant wealth and growing economic influence. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Entering the age of the older consumer As America ages, older consumers have more leisure time, significant wealth and growing economic influence. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

13023135     1

Economy 
Entering the age of the older consumer 
01 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• The older consumer is moving center stage. America's population has been aging for decades, but the shift has accelerated: the 
share aged over 60 increased by almost 10 percentage points (pp) between 1995 and 2025. And the Census Bureau projects the 
share of the population over 60 will rise another 5pp by 2055, to stand at nearly 30% of the population. 
• More free time means a different spending mix. People aged 65 and over spend around two and a half fewer hours per day 
working and roughly two hours more on leisure than the overall adult population. Bank of America data shows that older 
households allocate relatively more of their card spending to groceries and travel, and less to restaurants, gasoline, general 
merchandise and clothing. 
• Households aged over 55 held close to $140 trillion in net worth in the second quarter of 2026, giving many older consumers 
considerable capacity to spend on travel and other leisure categories. But wealth is not evenly distributed, and healthcare and 
other cost pressures remain significant for older households on tighter or fixed incomes.

Population aging is nothing new, but it is accelerating  
The US population has been growing relatively “older” for a long time. Exhibit 1 shows that the proportion of the population 
under 20 years old shrank from the mid-1960s through 2025. At the same time, the proportion of the population in older age 
groups has been rising.  
Exhibit 1: Over time the US population has steadily aged  
Proportion of US population by age range (%)

Source: Human Mortality Database; Bank of America Institute calculations

### Page 2

  I NSTI TUTE

Additionally, there has been a notable acceleration in the relative aging of the population in recent years. For example, between 
1995 and 2025, the proportion of the population aged over 60 rose by almost 10 percentage points (pp) to 25%.  
Several factors have driven this. First, fertility rates in the US, as in much of the world, have fallen sharply. This has slowed 
natural population growth and, by reducing the number of younger people relative to older cohorts, has increased the share of 
the population at older ages. Second, more recently, net inward migration to the US has slowed. Because migrants tend to be 
younger than the overall population, weaker immigration also tends to raise the average age. Finally, rising life expectancy over 
much of the post-war period has allowed more people to survive into older age, further increasing the older population's share.  
Exhibit 2: The trend towards an older composition of the population is expected to continue  
Forecast changes in the share of population by age group relative to 2025 (pp)

Source: United States Census Bureau 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Lower fertility and, more recently, weaker net migration look set to slow overall US population growth in the decades ahead. As a 
result, older Americans are likely to play an increasingly important role in the economy, both as a share of the population and, in 
many age groups, in absolute numbers. The Census Bureau projects the share of the population over 60 will rise another 5pp by 
2055, to stand at nearly 30% of the population (Exhibit 2). For businesses, understanding how the needs and spending patterns 
of older consumers differ from those of younger generations will therefore become increasingly relevant. 
Older age groups allocate their time differently  
Why does this matter? One reason is that older people tend to spend their time and money quite differently from younger adults. 
Most obviously, many work less or have retired altogether. Data from the American Time Use Survey (ATUS) shows that people 
aged 65 and over spend, on average, around two and a half fewer hours per day working than the population aged 15+ (Exhibit 
3). 
That leaves considerably more time for other activities. People aged 65 and over spend around two hours more per day on 
leisure and sports activities, with television viewing accounting for a particularly large share of this additional leisure time 
(Exhibit 4).

Older households also spend differently  
Bank of America internal data on credit and debit card spending provides insights into how these differences in older 
generations’ use of time also impact their spending. Rather than splitting our spending data by generation, as we often do, here 
we split by age bands, allowing for consistent comparisons over time.  
Exhibit 5 shows how the composition of card spending of households aged 61-75 and those older than 75 differs from that of all 
households. A few things stand out. For one, older households devote a greater share of their overall card spending to groceries 
(food and beverages). Households aged 61-75 also allocate more spending to travel (airlines and lodging). And restaurants and 
bars, gasoline, general merchandise and clothing account for a smaller share of older households’ card spending.  
These differences may seem fairly intuitive. Older households have more leisure hours, so spend relatively more on travel to 
enjoy them. Previously we found that more available time may support travel particularly around retirement, although the 
discretionary spending lift can be temporary (for more, read our publication: Paychecks to pensions: The evolution of retiree 
spending).  
Older households are also likely to have less need to update their work wardrobes, so they probably spend less on clothing.

Still, older households’ relatively lower spending on restaurants and bars is a little more curious, perhaps, given dining out could 
be one way of making use of more leisure time. Indeed, when we look at how these spending shares are evolving, we find a 
nuanced picture. While households aged 61-75 do spend relatively less on restaurants and bars than the overall population, they 
appear to be narrowing this gap. But for those older than 75 this is not the case. In our view, the pandemic may still be weighing 
on dining-out spending among this older age group (Exhibit 6).

Aging presents opportunities, but also challenges 
In our view, the aging of the US population presents important opportunities for the economy and businesses. Older consumers 
have more leisure time and businesses that cater to these demands may therefore benefit. 
Moreover, some older consumers hold considerable amounts of net wealth and have been some of the biggest beneficiaries of 
recent rises in equities. Using data from the Federal Reserve, Exhibit 8 shows that as of the second quarter of 2026 the net 
worth of those households aged 55 and over was close to $140 trillion, around three-quarters of the total. And with equity 
markets rising strongly, these age groups have seen a more than 20% increase in their net worth over the past two years, 
potentially enhancing their spending power further.  
But an important caveat is that not all older consumers are in this position. Previous Institute work found evidence consistent 
with equity-market wealth effects being concentrated toward higher-income households (read our publication: Paychecks to 
pensions: The evolution of retiree spending). And Census Bureau research has found that around 14% of Social Security 
recipients aged over 65 rely on it for over 90% of their income. For older households without a wealth cushion, recent elevated 
cost pressures, like gasoline, will likely have put them under more significant financial strain. They will also be more dependent 
on cost-of-living (COLA) increases in Social Security to underpin their spending.  
And, more broadly, outside of card spending, healthcare costs are a much larger factor in older households’ budgets (Exhibit 9). 
The evolution of these costs going forward will be a key variable for older consumers’ budgets.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts.  
Durables spending is defined as spending on electronics, building materials, auto-related spending and furniture. Premium durables spending is 
based on a selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively higher value products. Conversely, value durables spending is based on a 
selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively lower value products.  
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

EUROZONE: IMPROVING BUSINESS SENTIMENT, DRIVEN BY TECH, IS SPREADING TO OTHER SECTORS
Stéphane Colliac & Tarik Rharrab
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
The September PMI surveys confirm that growth in the Eurozone is getting stronger, boosted in particular by new export orders, which are
still rising. Sectors that were already performing well – technology and capital goods (including for defence) – are continuing to grow, while

those that had been struggling until now – aeronautics, consumer goods and financial services – are seeing their production indices recover.
This strong performance is particularly noteworthy, given that it has not yet been undermined by higher inflation.
The heatmap shows the PMI index readings: a reading above 50 indicates expansion, while a reading below 50 signals contraction compared to the previous
month. For each indicator, sectors are ranked in descending order based on their September 2026 reading.

Shades of red represent values above 50, while shades of blue represent values below 50: the intensity of the colour increases the further away they are
from this threshold.
Production growth spreads to more sectors
The PMI surveys on business sentiment confirm that business activity in the Eurozone is expanding at a faster pace and spreading to more sectors.
Household-consumption-related sectors (goods and services), which were adversely affected in the spring by the outbreak of the conflict in the Middle

East, do not appear to be negatively impacted by the rising inflation observed since August. The aeronautics sector, where production has mainly been
limited by supply constraints, is also showing signs of improvement.
SOURCE: S&P GLOBAL, BNP PARIBAS
